# OpenAlex lead architecture coverage register (Sections 1–30)

> **Register for [#122] (DE-01).** Built from `main` @ `2e785fd186f70c9a591ca0d77387a4e8f52a9db9` on 2026-10-13; rows updated on 2026-10-14 for [#123] (DE-02) and [#124] (DE-03); rows updated on 2026-10-16 for [#126] (DE-05); rows updated on 2026-10-17 for [#127] (DE-06); rows updated on 2026-10-17 for [#128] (DE-07). Every atomic requirement of the lead's *OpenAlex Data Storage, Query, and Serving Architecture* (Sections 1–30, including 3.1, 6.1, 6.2, 8.1–8.4 and 10.1) has one row that names the prior issues and merged PRs, the files and tests that implement it, how strongly it is proven, what is missing, and which open issue owns the rest.

This register is a traceability record, not an approval. It does not authorize cloud work, deployments, new feeds or an AWS implementation. Where it and a detailed page disagree, the code, the tests and the dated *Recorded run* sections win; fix the row. For a narrative summary of the same evidence see [Status](../overview/status.md).

## How to read this register

- **ID** is `L<section>[.<subsection>]-<sequence>`, numbered in the lead's own order. **Requirement** quotes the lead's wording for one sentence, list item, field, query, endpoint or technology candidate. Technology candidates are prefixed `GCP example:` or `AWS example:`.
- **Prior issues / merged PRs** lists closed issues below [#122] and merged PRs only. Planned work is in **Follow-on**, as the DE planning ID and its GitHub issue (DE-*k* is issue #(121+*k*); umbrella [#157]).
- **Paths** and **Tests / evidence** cite tracked files and pytest node IDs, or the doc or issue comment that records a cloud run. A CI test checks that each one exists.
- An architecture decision record in **Paths** (for example [ADR 0001](../adr/0001-gcp-architecture-and-service-selection.md)) means the row's gap names a recorded decision. A decision is not evidence: it never raises **Evidence** or **Status**, and the implementing work stays in **Follow-on**. The [ADR index](../adr/README.md) gives each ADR's status.

**Class**

| Class | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `MANDATORY` | The lead states it as a requirement or principle that should hold. |
| `CONDITIONAL` | The lead makes it depend on measurement, justification or a future need. |
| `ILLUSTRATIVE` | The lead calls it an example: technology candidates, sample SQL, sample endpoints. An adopted example stays illustrative; its status shows the adoption. |

**Evidence** (strongest level truthfully shown for that row; never upgraded by implication)

| Evidence | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `DEFINED` | Code, SQL or a typed contract exists on `main` and offline deterministic tests in default CI exercise it (local, in-memory, DuckDB or fake GCS/BigQuery). Says nothing about the cloud. |
| `SEMANTIC_ONLY` | Stated only in docs, ADR text, YAML configuration or a placeholder. Nothing executable enforces it. |
| `CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED` | Ran against real GCP services in an operator-controlled project (the `GCP_SANDBOX`, or the personal DEV project for the [#118] lookup runs) on synthetic or public-sample data, and the result was recorded: the [#91]/[#92] smokes, [#103], [#108] Stages 1–2 and [#118]. A measurement, not an acceptance. |
| `PERSONAL_DEV_QA_VERIFIED` | [#108] Stage 3 re-runs of the Stage 1 checks on the same synthetic data (synthetic ~10k Works) in operator-controlled **personal** projects labelled DEV and QA. Not enterprise evidence, not real OpenAlex data, and no more representative in scale than `CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED` (the largest run, about 100k synthetic Works, was [#108] Stage 2 in the sandbox). |
| `MISSING` | Nothing on `main` implements or demonstrates it. |
| `CONDITIONAL_DEFERRED` | The lead makes it optional and a recorded decision or gate defers it (for example [#23] closed as not planned). |
| `NOT_APPLICABLE` | Only for `OUT_OF_SCOPE` rows (AWS examples). |

`ENTERPRISE_DEV_VERIFIED`, `ENTERPRISE_QA_VERIFIED` and `PROD_DEPLOYED` are reserved. No enterprise-managed environment or production deployment exists, so no row may use them; the CI test rejects them until a PR records that evidence.

**Status**

| Status | Meaning | Follow-on |
|---|---|---|
| `MET` | Satisfied for this repository's current scope at the stated evidence level. | none |
| `PARTIAL` | Some implementation or evidence exists; the rest is open. | required |
| `NOT_MET` | Not implemented. | required |
| `DEFERRED` | Conditional item deliberately deferred by a recorded decision or measurement gate. | optional (the gate issue) |
| `OUT_OF_SCOPE` | AWS examples and anything outside the OpenAlex-on-GCP program. No ticket is created. | none |

**Owner** is a role, not a person: Data Engineering, Lead architect, Cloud platform, Security and governance, API product owner, or Downstream owners (post-G5).

## Special decisions

The three recorded decisions or measurement campaigns that most change how lead requirements are scored. Results are taken from the issue comments (GitHub REST) and the repo at 2e785fd; tracked docs lag the issue record for all three, most for [#23] and [#118].

| Decision | What was decided or measured (with evidence level and environment label) | Recorded where | Lead sections affected | Consequence for this register | Follow-on |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [#23] optional PostgreSQL/AlloyDB serving projection: closed not_planned 2026-10-13 (06:42 UTC) by operator decision | Operator chose the BigQuery + API/cache path for interactive DOI and work-ID lookups; the gate (BigQuery + API/cache must measurably miss a stated requirement) was not met, so no PostgreSQL/AlloyDB projection is adopted: CONDITIONAL_DEFERRED. Inputs: the operator-stated requirement recorded 2026-10-12 by [#119] (interactive app/API, at most 200 ms end-to-end p95, at most 5 rps, daily freshness; SEMANTIC_ONLY config, not a measured or product-validated Wiley SLA) and the [#118] runs (CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED, personal DEV). The [#112] local PostgreSQL 16 PROTOTYPE stays unapproved and undeployed: DEFINED (offline unit tests in default CI; 9 integration tests only against local Docker PostgreSQL, 20 passed 2026-10-11); AlloyDB, latency, concurrency and scale MISSING. Earlier gate evaluations on 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12 also recorded not met | Issue record: [#23] comments (gate evaluation and inputs 2026-10-11; prototype status, [#108] note and requirement recorded 2026-10-12; closing decision 2026-10-13) and the [#2] body and closing comment. Repo: requirement in `config/query-patterns/openalex.yaml` ([#119]; placement still BIGQUERY_PLUS_CACHE, evidence still ASSUMED; both lookups' placement_rationale cites the [#118] runs and the closure). Tracked docs now record the not_planned closure, among them `docs/architecture/serving-work-lookup-projection.md` (links the closing comment; requirement 200 ms p95, 5 rps, 24 h freshness), `docs/overview/status.md` ([#23] and [#2] Closed), `docs/architecture/environment-readiness.md` (reopen criteria), `README.md`, `docs/architecture/roadmap.md` and `docs/architecture/query-routing.md` | 1-4, 6-10, 12-27, 29, 30 (rows citing [#23]); CONDITIONAL_DEFERRED store rows in 2, 7, 10, 12, 15, 17-24, 27, 29, 30 | Dedicated operational-store rows (PostgreSQL, Cloud SQL, AlloyDB) are CONDITIONAL_DEFERRED, not MISSING; the [#112] prototype cannot raise them above DEFINED. Serving-layer rows in 1, 2, 7 and 8 are judged on the BigQuery + in-process cache path (PARTIAL at best; cache CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED in personal DEV only). A production cache is MISSING (DE-30); eISSN/ISSN lookup stays MISSING (DE-29). Reopen: over 5% warm-cache misses, or a shared cache breaking the 200 ms p95 | DE-29, DE-30, DE-35 |
| [#108] GCS/BigQuery validation beyond the POC sandbox: closed completed 2026-10-12 (20:31 UTC); an operator decision that day accepted the POC-project evidence for Stages 1-2 in place of an enterprise-managed project | Stage 0: offline tooling [#109], [#110], [#111], [#114] (DEFINED). Stage 1, GCP_SANDBOX, as a least-privilege service account reached by impersonation (no key files): GCS smoke 17/17, BigQuery smoke 10/10, [#103] deploy and measure 95/95 at synthetic-10010-works (run i103-20261012t031724); CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED. Stage 2, GCP_SANDBOX: attempt i103-20261012t043457 failed on a script cap defect fixed by [#114], then i103-20261012t180931 passed 95/95 at synthetic-100100-works; clustering OBSERVED for 4 of 11 patterns, with a reduction only for citation_relationships (0.157 of the dry-run bytes); CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED. Stage 3: the same three runs passed in personal DEV (95/95, run i103-20261012t193514) and then in personal QA with a separate service account (95/95, run i103-20261012t200938; the DEV account was refused QA access): PERSONAL_DEV_QA_VERIFIED (the repo registry says VERIFIED for GCS_RAW_LANDING and BIGQUERY_ANALYTICAL in DEV and QA). The timeout probe completed each time, so cancellation was never exercised | Issue record: [#108] comments (Stage 1 interim 2026-10-11; Stage 1, Stage 2, operator decision, Stage 3 DEV, Stage 3 QA and closing, all 2026-10-12). Tracked docs: Recorded run sections for Stages 1, 2, 3 DEV and 3 QA in `docs/architecture/bigquery-analytical-models.md` and for Stages 1, 3 DEV and 3 QA in `docs/architecture/immutable-local-landing.md`; levels in `src/research_platform/readiness/registry.py` ([#113], [#115], [#116], [#117]); summary in `docs/overview/status.md`, which also records the closure. `docs/architecture/environment-readiness.md`, `README.md` and `docs/architecture/roadmap.md` show GCS_RAW_LANDING and BIGQUERY_ANALYTICAL VERIFIED in GCP_SANDBOX, DEV and QA at domain level only, with every environment NOT_READY; `tests/unit/test_readiness_docs_truth.py` checks those docs and `docs/overview/status.md` against the registry. Lag: the POC-acceptance decision is only on the issue | 1-12, 14-30 (rows citing [#108]) | Rows citing [#108] top out at CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED (Stages 1-2) or PERSONAL_DEV_QA_VERIFIED (Stage 3), never ENTERPRISE or PROD. Still MISSING: enterprise-managed sandbox, DEV and QA; separate deploy and runtime identities (IDENTITY_IAM stays CLOUD_UNVERIFIED); cancellation under load; a representative OpenAlex slice; production-scale cost and latency; concurrent publication; Gold refresh and materialization; MERGE/REPLACE beyond works and work_topics. Every environment stays NOT_READY, PROD BLOCKED, can_promote refused | DE-04, DE-13, DE-14, DE-17, DE-18, DE-22, DE-23, DE-34 |
| [#118] BigQuery + in-process cache measurement for DOI and work-ID lookups against the operator-stated 200 ms p95: closed completed 2026-10-13 (06:42 UTC) | Four runs, all in the personal DEV project as its least-privilege service account, over the synthetic-10k-works tables of the Stage 3 [#103] run; Zipf(1.1) over 1,000 ACTIVE works, seed 118, 500 warm-up plus 2,000 measured requests, in-process TtlLruCache (24 h TTL, 1,000 entries); CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED, a measurement and not an acceptance. Run 1 (2026-10-12): DOI, cold cache (warm-up only, no prewarm), 85.7% hits, overall p95 2,426 ms: target MISSED. Run 2 (2026-10-13): DOI, prewarmed, 100% hits, p95 0.006 ms: target MET. Run 3 (2026-10-13): work-ID, cold, 85.7% hits, p95 2,554 ms: target MISSED. Run 4 (2026-10-13): work-ID, prewarmed, 100% hits, p95 0.010 ms: target MET. Cold-run miss p95 was 3,021 ms (DOI) and 3,097 ms (work-ID); 28.6 GiB billed in total. Rule from the runs: the 200 ms p95 is met if and only if under 5% of requests miss | Run 1: [#118] comment (2026-10-13 03:45 UTC), the commit ff82034 message and the PR [#121] body. Run 2: [#118] comment (04:47 UTC). Runs 3 and 4: [#118] closing comment (06:42 UTC). All four are summarized in the [#23] closing comment, the [#2] body and tracked docs that cite the issue comments as the record: a four-run table in `docs/architecture/serving-work-lookup-projection.md` ([#118] BigQuery + cache measurement), a runs 1-4 row in the `docs/overview/status.md` validation history and a summary in `docs/architecture/query-pattern-benchmarks.md` (Evidence classification); `README.md` gives the four p95 values. `config/query-patterns/openalex.yaml` cites the cold and prewarmed p95 in both lookups' placement_rationale and keeps evidence_status ASSUMED | 1, 2, 7-10, 12-15, 17-27, 29, 30 (rows citing [#118]) | DOI and work-ID lookup-latency rows may cite CLOUD_SYNTHETIC_MEASURED (personal DEV, synthetic data) and must state both the cold MISS and the prewarmed MEET. The real working set, a shared or production cache (its network hop was not measured), concurrency and real-data scale stay MISSING. The cache code is DEFINED; the run record is the [#118] issue comments, summarized in `docs/architecture/serving-work-lookup-projection.md`, `docs/overview/status.md` and `docs/architecture/query-pattern-benchmarks.md` | DE-18, DE-26, DE-28, DE-30 |

No real OpenAlex payload has been landed (one Works pair was retrieved only in the DE-05 LOCAL bounded inspection), and no full snapshot, incremental manifest watermark or non-Works entity has been acquired; the source manifest is not persisted. Acquire-once semantics are defined. Real manifests carry no checksum; landing records the SHA-256 of the retrieved bytes; the DE-05 inspection recorded S3 ETag and CRC64NVME for the profiled pair and the ledgers; since [#127] the per-asset landing manifest records the size, MD5 and SHA-256 of each landed object and, for streams that report response metadata (the OpenAlex connector), its Content-Length, `etag` and `version_id`; landing refuses a byte count unlike the declared size (for streams that report it, and on the GCS backend for any asset with a known size), an MD5 unlike a single-part ETag, and a gzip payload that ends inside a member. Interrupted retrieval is compensated by a whole-file restart with idempotent landing ([#126]); range resume stays deferred under the 25,000,000-byte cap ([#127]). | DE-05 [#126]; DE-06 [#127]; DE-11 [#132] | Data Engineering |

## Traceability matrices

Cross-cutting, item-by-item views of the same evidence are on a companion page, [traceability matrices](openalex-lead-coverage-matrices.md): lead entities (§4) and analytical retention (§9), serving fields (§8), API examples (§13, §16), routing (§15), query patterns (§6, §11, §19, §21, §23, §25), aggregates (§12), the full issue and PR crosswalk and the repository file inventory.

## Maintaining this register

- Update the affected rows in the same PR that changes the code, tests or evidence. A PR that closes a follow-on issue narrows the gap of its rows, moves a row to `MET` only when the lead requirement as written is fully satisfied, and drops that follow-on.
- Rows owned by *Downstream owners (post-G5)* (enrichment, knowledge graph, data science, agents, ARI) never move to `MET` through a DE issue: DE-32 only narrows their gap to the published data-product interface. Their implementation is downstream work, not DE work.
- `tests/unit/test_lead_coverage_register.py` runs in `make test`. It fails on a missing section or subsection, an unknown class, evidence, status or owner, a reserved enterprise or production evidence label, a cited path or test function that does not exist, a follow-on whose issue number does not match its DE ID, an AWS example with a ticket, an issue or PR reference without a link, and a dropped lead entity, field, query, endpoint or technology candidate.
- Keep private identifiers out: no project IDs, bucket names or account emails. The repository and the docs site are public.
- When the lead revises the architecture, re-run the audit against the new text and record the new source version here.
